Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Les_Paul's Journal 09 Dec 2013 09:58 #278 Hotochin --> Offline Trader Posts: 588 Thank you received: 729 Hey Guys, Harry----This is something that you wrote in an above post. -"but you dont need to learn everything to be able to trade, if you master one setup you can pretty much use that all the time. some master traders here in RTM have loved one setup and use that all the time." What is hard for me with this statement is how to know what a "set up" is. The Marketpedia section is wonderful at explaining many different things, but there is really no point at which to start. Its hard to know what to look for where. There are QM,engulf's, FO, CP, and diamonds all over the place. And once you narrow those down, do I always need a FL? How about a QM? You see what I mean? Its like if I took a car apart for you and you knew nothing about cars. You are looking at parts all in front of you. Then I told you to start with the transmission. Now I just gave you the answer as to how to start. The problem is that you don't know what a transmission is anyways. So even though you have an answer, it really does not help. I tried my best to explain my issue here, hopefully you understand what I am saying. Any thoughts? H Check this box to be notified of replies to this topic. Note: BBcode and smileys are still usable. Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Les_Paul's Journal 09 Dec 2013 11:23 #279 Aaron --> Offline Associate Trader Posts: 668 Thank you received: 1593 Scott, I hope you don't mind me giving my view on this buddy. What I think Harry means when he says that, is, take for instance a textbook engulf setup, price is heading to supply, price on the LTFs engulf an important low indicating bearish intention and then take the RT to a cap or FTR, this is a set up I love seeing and I am seeing it more and more. The QM could be a setup on its own as long as you are using the HTF S/D zones etc... One could just look for a fake out in a HTF area etc... I think the thing to do, and which has helped me a lot is study one setup and study it till u know it inside out, the you will understand what Harry means mate. I have tried to explain how I think, but as you know no trader is the same, so take this with an open mind my friend. DT Check this box to be notified of replies to this topic. Its like if I took a car apart for you and you knew nothing about cars. You are looking at parts all in front of you. Then I told you to start with the transmission. Now I just gave you the answer as to how to start. The problem is that you don't know what a transmission is anyways. So even though you have an answer, it really does not help. I tried my best to explain my issue here, hopefully you understand what I am saying. Any thoughts? H Hotochin, my friend! I hear a lot of frustration in your voice. The frustration is understandable, I have been through a LOT of FX frustration even after joining RTM! However, the frustration is not necessary. If we look to other sucessful traders here, we can be sure that it can be done! Concerning what you have written: I got your point! But imagine it as a menu in such a kind of restaurant where you can set up your menu on your own from available ingredients. You can decide what side dish, what kind of meat, etc etc etc. Here it is the same. You have individual components and it is up to you what dish you will create. So, let's choose one setup! In the following picture you can see my PL curve from Forex Tester 1. Looks good, doesn't it? I concentrated only on one setup: 1: I identified H4 FTR's and price caps (all of them). 2: I waited for price to engulf a level and return to one below above.(here, the main difference between FT1 and real time trading shows: in FT1 you can fast forward PA so you don't have to wait days... and it is also the main reason I cannot reach such success in real life: I AM IMPATIENT!!!! ) 3: So once PA got into a H4 level, I switched to M15 and waited for a QM or break and retest of M15 S/R line. 4: TP - opposite H4 level, SL - some 15 - 20 pips below/above pivot Of course, the PL curve shown above is UNREALISTIC for me in real life due to my lack of patience.... but I am working on it! Also, well, I knew there would be a big drop in 2008 in advance... so I could act accordingly and concentrate on shorts mainly! So my dear Hotochin, if you find the setup interesting, try to find some examples and you can put them here in my journal or in yours... Let's work on it together mate! In real life I am very impatient (I have been working on this issue of mine for a couple of years now and must admit that I have made a substantial progress, but still, I am far from ideal state), so I hardly ever wait for a proper setup... SO, the answer to my personal struggle is: ALWAYS WAIT FOR THE PRICE ACTION TO SHOW YOU THE WAY (universal RTM rule), and ALWAYS WAIT FOR YOUR SWEET FTR!!!!! (my personal extremely englightening rule) I hope I haven't disappointed you very much - I know it's nothing new, but for me it is a huge leap forward.... The following user(s) said Thank You: Les_Paul , cuimingzhao Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Les_Paul's Journal 19 Dec 2013 01:22 #345 Spongiform --> Offline Trader Posts: 196 Thank you received: 273 Hello Les_Paul, I love what you're doing with 'sweet FTRs'. I've been following a similar line of thought for a while, what I call 'trading in the shadow of the reaction to longer time-frame Supply or Demand'. I seem to keep mangling this on the hard right edge: I think it's been down to a lack of direction, and spending too much time in the detail rather than stepping back and watching the big picture. I'm a little late to the party, but I was particularly interested in an FTR you pointed out (in post #317) in response to a question from Daft trader 1 (in post #316). Fxyogi sort of picked up on it in post #340. Here's your chart with the FTR in orange: Warning: Spoiler! [ Click to expand ] [ Click to hide ] I'm sure it's a matter of what your rules say, but why did you pick that one over the lower one? I preferred the lower FTR but the reasoning is discretionary. The difference between our choices is the difference between viewing that spike down as an EG of one FTR or a reaction to the other (and thereby directional bias for potential trades). This is not to say I made the better choice etc (and a visit to my journal shows my preference for making the wrong choice as often as humanly possible ), but to understand the different philosophies and to learn... . For comparison, here are some charts explaining how I thought both FTRs (hindsight being 20 : 20 and all that, but I would have been attracted to the one I've marked regardless). Warning: Spoiler! [ Click to expand ] [ Click to hide ] H4 - first up, ancient history to give context to the PA of interest: H4 - two views of the PA of interest. It shows your higher FTR and my lower FTR. Mine occurs as a result of most recent PA, (to me) yours seems to occur at an area from ancient history rather than a break above recent Supply (there are no orders from that time in the market, and information quality degrades over time). That higher FTR occurs at an interesting area. Market orders (Buy Stops and breakout trader buys) are triggered right into that ancient Supply: this would have been a great place for an FO if Pro Money wanted to move price down (having plundered liquidity to fill short orders) but instead we see price grind higher (CP) immediately after. That CP suggests hard work being done, and that suggests orders being filled. This combined with my thoughts that this is a reaction to ancient history (i.e. questionable) makes the upper FTR (which I thought of as more of a DP, but I'm sure that's semantics) less utile than the lower (in my opinion!). By comparison that lower one looks like it had an easy life: H4 - for fun, a reminder that it's always advisable to look to the left for Stop placement on FTRs W1 - and for more fun, stepping back would have shown a Flag in the making, a pullback into Demand for a move higher (again, with the joy of 20 : 20 hindsight): Thanks in advance. Spongy. Attachments: Check this box to be notified of replies to this topic. Note: BBcode and smileys are still usable. Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Les_Paul's Journal 19 Dec 2013 07:00 #349 Les_Paul Topic Author --> Offline Master Trader Posts: 554 Thank you received: 1491 Spongiform wrote: Hello Les_Paul, I love what you're doing with 'sweet FTRs'. I've been following a similar line of thought for a while, what I call 'trading in the shadow of the reaction to longer time-frame Supply or Demand'. I seem to keep mangling this on the hard right edge: I think it's been down to a lack of direction, and spending too much time in the detail rather than stepping back and watching the big picture. I'm a little late to the party, but I was particularly interested in an FTR you pointed out (in post #317) in response to a question from Daft trader 1 (in post #316). Fxyogi sort of picked up on it in post #340. Here's your chart with the FTR in orange: Warning: Spoiler! [ Click to expand ] [ Click to hide ] I'm sure it's a matter of what your rules say, but why did you pick that one over the lower one? I preferred the lower FTR but the reasoning is discretionary. The difference between our choices is the difference between viewing that spike down as an EG of one FTR or a reaction to the other (and thereby directional bias for potential trades). This is not to say I made the better choice etc (and a visit to my journal shows my preference for making the wrong choice as often as humanly possible ), but to understand the different philosophies and to learn... . For comparison, here are some charts explaining how I thought both FTRs (hindsight being 20 : 20 and all that, but I would have been attracted to the one I've marked regardless). Warning: Spoiler! [ Click to expand ] [ Click to hide ] H4 - first up, ancient history to give context to the PA of interest: H4 - two views of the PA of interest. It shows your higher FTR and my lower FTR. Mine occurs as a result of most recent PA, (to me) yours seems to occur at an area from ancient history rather than a break above recent Supply (there are no orders from that time in the market, and information quality degrades over time). That higher FTR occurs at an interesting area. Market orders (Buy Stops and breakout trader buys) are triggered right into that ancient Supply: this would have been a great place for an FO if Pro Money wanted to move price down (having plundered liquidity to fill short orders) but instead we see price grind higher (CP) immediately after. That CP suggests hard work being done, and that suggests orders being filled. This combined with my thoughts that this is a reaction to ancient history (i.e. questionable) makes the upper FTR (which I thought of as more of a DP, but I'm sure that's semantics) less utile than the lower (in my opinion!). By comparison that lower one looks like it had an easy life: H4 - for fun, a reminder that it's always advisable to look to the left for Stop placement on FTRs W1 - and for more fun, stepping back would have shown a Flag in the making, a pullback into Demand for a move higher (again, with the joy of 20 : 20 hindsight): Thanks in advance. Spongy. Dear Spongy, this is how I see it - I am learning to keep it simple... LP Attachments: Check this box to be notified of replies to this topic. Note: BBcode and smileys are still usable. I registered in January 2013, but at that time I was focused in other things so I didn't pay attention to the necessary process of learning. This is also an FTR. Why you didn't take into account this? Look at the swing points that define FTR1. The HH at the right is also the first Swing High of FTR2. I can see two inner ftrs (ftrs within FTR1). The right leg of the second one is the one that defines FTR1. FTR PAZ is defined by FTR1 and FTR2 About Engulfs: Post readthemarket.com/index.php/forum/journa...rnal?start=400#35777 I have some doubts about how you choose which level have to be engulfed in order to use it as confirmation. Think in terms of Flag Limits: :Picture a Rally - Base - Rally Or Pole - FTR - Flagging if you like. When price breaks the limit, it will likely fill the base. Now look at what was engulfed in those charts and then look at the distance to the base. If price engulfs the limit and retraces, entry is at DP to engulf, and target is the base of the pole. Hope this helps. Attachments: Check this box to be notified of replies to this topic. Note: BBcode and smileys are still usable. The following user(s) said Thank You: cuimingzhao Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Les_Paul's Journal 13 Jun 2016 15:06 #418 Bodostock --> Offline Trader Posts: 214 Thank you received: 224 Hi, Les Paul Sorry to post this in your journal. But because this was your posting which i have some queries, i thought it would be better to post it here , hoping to get some feedback from anyone. In posting 174, you have shared your knowledge of PAZ - compression, stacked supply/demand and zone between PAZ. I couldn't understand the concept of stacked supply/ demand PAZ and how to really spot them. And for another reason i do not understand is that unlike compression where supply/demand was already consumed while on the way up or down to the the next DP, ity was said that stacked supply and demand still has its unfilled order left behind. So i do not understand the rationale that when the the edge of the SPAZ was engulfed, how is it that we can be so sure it will reach the other end of the PAZ? I mean since there are unfilled orders at every stacked supply/demand, shouldn't we be expecting reaction at one of the stack that could possibly bring price back to the original direction? I have this thinking because since stacked supply and demand has unfilled orders and they carry on to move lower /higher, aren't they considered as FTR and if they are considered as FTR, that's the more i feel that price would have reaction when they retraced to the stacks rather than engulfing every one of them all the way to the other end. Then on this example Les Paul shown in his post 174, he showed this chart as an example of a SPAZ and how it was engulfed. Now as per i added my comments in the chart, to me i am not sure how this looks like a stacked supply. I thought it was a engulf of low, then price retraced but capped formed a RBD FTR. Then as price bounced back later, it was at least to me that price was resisted by the FTR and came down. But i am surprised to see that Les Paul treated this contact as being an engulf of the zone. Of course, price eventually did go back to the very top in the end. But i just could not understand how was that an engulf. The following user(s) said Thank You: cuimingzhao Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Les_Paul's Journal 08 Dec 2016 17:25 #437 moogroo --> Offline Trader Posts: 151 Thank you received: 297 Hi Supreme, I just noticed you didn't get a response while re-reading LPs posts here, and forex tester is something I use quite a lot. I personally subscribe to the VIP data package, and update the data almost on a daily basis. You can use the ECNfeed option to get good quality tick data up to something like 2013 (which I found to be the best), and then I just use FXCM data from then on whenever I update the data. Alternatively, I know people who get their data from a service like Dukascopy and import data into Forex Tester themselves. Everyone has their own way of learning along with the tools that they use, and FT is by no means a necessity. But I like being able to drill down into lower time frames in the distant past, and if I spot an interesting chart someone posted from 2012-2013 for example, I can go and investigate and play through it without any issue, whatever the time frame may be! Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Les_Paul's Journal 04 May 2013 18:37 #56 Infest --> Offline Associate Trader Reach perfection. Posts: 230 Thank you received: 412 Great start, Les_Paul. I will also post a few more setups of this weeks trading session - mostly homework at entries that would have been good to enter but I didnt because I was not around or not aware of the setup. This can also be used for trend continuation. It is kind of quasimodo within the trend. Works best if the the countertrend-swing bounced off unimportant levels, so the probability to enter at the setup level is the highest because the trend is always your friend ;-) But a trend also ends at some point, and most of the time this happens at HTF levels. But since I also like to trade countertrend on HTF, it is a good method to get in when an engulf happens at M30 or higher at a HTF supply or demand zone. Thats a good sign that pro money is active and they want to retest a decision point to confirm the trend reversal. If you look through your examples, you see that most of the time, a touch trade just doesn't make sense, because price ITSELF tells you time is right to step on the train when it engulfs previous swing levels. So instead of trying to catch tops and bottoms, it's better to wait for this setup to happen so that we can enter on the retest of a broken level. Also important. If you look through your examples, you see that the REAL move in the opposite direction happens AFTER the retest. So there is no reason to be nervous to miss the move. Price itself will tell you when it's time to change direction. This is something very important I have learned in the last weeks and months. For me, there is only one level I would enter with a pending order right now: If the level is a) a HTF level, b) the turning point of a big swing, c) lots of time went past and d) the level is fresh and has a clear decision point (S/R flip) to enter at. Especially midlevels, such as DBD or RBR on HTF always need this kind of setup confirmation first in order to trade the reversals. I just got stopped out too many times and this prevented me from growing my account. Check this box to be notified of replies to this topic. Note: BBcode and smileys are still usable. Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Les_Paul's Journal 09 May 2013 02:51 #71 sunnytrader1984 --> Offline Associate Trader Posts: 375 Thank you received: 731 Hey rbnj, Yes, I took the trade. Yes it failed. Did I do something wrong? No, I kept the loss small, it could have worked, it could not have...and it didnt. So, why it did not work....zoom out a little, go to Daily, you will see where the price finally stopped. There was a more significant supply up there, which price went for. There is something for all of us to understand, if there are stacked demand or supply, price would almost always go for the last zone. Want to see it for yourself, take GU chart, open H1 and mark all FTRs and then see the price moving up today. You will find 4 FTRs, and see where the price turned from. here is a chart about it. Always be mindful of HTF supply or Demand above or below your zone, if there is one, price will try to come in to that level. Take care and have a nice evening. Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Les_Paul's Journal 23 May 2013 04:16 #110 Hotochin --> Offline Trader Posts: 588 Thank you received: 729 Les_Paul wrote: this is how I see it so far: I would be curious what IF or someone else has to say about your zones. I think you have it! Me and you seem to be similar. You made a comment in another post about overcomplicating things. I tend to do the same. I am very analytical and have to have everything laid out. Trading is not always this way. I drew my zones the same way, but I found the opposing FL differently. Let me explain. In your diagram that I quoted above. Price is entering zone 3 from zone 2. I agree with how you found the upper FTR that formed the upper limit of zone 3. The thing I am curious about is the lower limit of zone 3. I see that in your drawing you waited for an engulf and FTR which created the lower limit. You drew the limit from the origin of the engulf though, not the FTR on the bottom. So the upper FL is from a FTR and the bottom is from the origin of an engulf. How I am thinking you find the lower limits is by looking far the the left in the past to "predict" where price will turn. In your scenario there is an engulf and FTR that makes the lower zone. Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Les_Paul's Journal 23 May 2013 09:47 #115 mettafort --> Offline Trader If I were a song...http://m.youtube.com/watch?v=ov9kH54Z-dM&desktop_uri=%2Fwatch%3Fv%3Dov9kH54Z-dM Posts: 327 Thank you received: 387 Hi Les_Paul and Hotochin, I just wanted to add something which I dont think is always obvious. When price breaks out and then retraces but holds under new demand that has formed, out from the top of the PAZ, like the first example in the chart LeS_Paul put togther, thats the failure-to-return - it tells us it couldnt return down because sitting here is fresh demand. When price breaks out of PAZ 2, there is a clear RBR - the B is the FL, and then price retraces, only for a bar. This bounces right above the FL - this is the FTR as far as I understand it, because its major purpose for being identified as a concept on the chart, is that it is a marker that supply or demand is now imbalanced in the opposing direction - it is another place that allows us to reason about order flow. And more subtely, to me an FTR is the marker that price failed to get back to the origin of the move when it retraced, because of order flow. I am sure you understand this actually, but thought maybe using some different verbiage might add to it. MF. Check this box to be notified of replies to this topic. Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Les_Paul's Journal 31 May 2013 02:39 #142 Les_Paul Topic Author --> Offline Master Trader Posts: 554 Thank you received: 1491 Dear Hotochin, I will try to explain my way of identifying PAZ's. I by no means claim that the way I do it is right cause I am still working hard on it. I must admit that I am doing this in hindsight, in real time I find it much harder. SCREEN 1 In the SCREEN 1 you can see that price makes low, turns, engulfs FL (dotted line) and makes FTR. Then it starts travelling. north. The newly formed FTR is now lower edge of my PAZ. Above, you can see an FTR that was mede on the previous way down. This upper FTR is my top edge of PAZ. Will price bounce or go through? Only PA in LTF can tell me. SCREEN 2 In SCREEN 2 you can see the price shot through the FTR, which means that its supply becomes demand. It is now my lower edge of the new PAZ. As far as the upper edge is concerned, it is formed by the nex FTR in line. What is PA going to do now? I don't know, I must wait and see. SCREEN 3 In SCREEN 3 we can see that PA raced to the upper edge, engulfed it a bit and got back into the PAZ. The bias is long after 2 engulfed levels in a couple of minutes. PA starts flagging. In this situation I took the failed EU trade. Had I done this analysis before? Well, no... If I had done it, I would have never taken the trade in the place I did. In M5 it can be nicely seen that PA engulfed Flag Limit and retraced to the origin of the move - upper edge of PAZ. Now it was apparent that price wanted to go down to that SUPPLY TURNED DEMAND level (lower edge of PAZ). In my opinion this was the moment for taking a scalp south. But I was not aware of the situation. Due to my stupidity I did not check the overal situation and took that trade - in the middle of that flag / range / whatever we call it. It was nobody's land - no trade should have been taken... Well I hope that what I have just written makes some sense. Les_Paul Attachments: Check this box to be notified of replies to this topic. Les_Paul, sorry to butt in here again, but I wanted to emphasize the importance of HTF-demand/supply regarding the succes rate of an engulf based trade: 1. UJ trade: looking at the 4H-TF we see that in june price engulfed a nice supply by a clean bullbar and after the engulf price made a very nice FTR just above the engulfed supply, we now have stacked demands ie. a nice beartrap configuration. The outcome was as already mentioned by Les_Paul, indeed a FO /engulf PA based trade on the LTF 2. EJ-trade: looking at the 6H-TF we see that in june price engulfed a nice supply, after the engulf price made a very nice FTR, its here were price reacted yesterday and if you switch to the LTF you'll see an engulf PA based trade. The engulf trade Myst mentioned wasn't valid in my opinion since price didn't reacted at a significant HTF demand, that demand was somewhat lower Attachments: Check this box to be notified of replies to this topic. Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Les_Paul's Journal 25 Sep 2013 16:54 #217 Les_Paul Topic Author --> Offline Master Trader Posts: 554 Thank you received: 1491 Hi! I want to show two setups here: in conventional terminology it is Demand-turned-supply zone (SWAP ZONE). However, behind this technocratic term there are novice traders who are trapped in losing positions: 1 - demand is created and people unaware of the fact that price just left HTF RBD zone buy. 2 - demand is engulfed and these people suddenly have losing positions 3 - price revisits zone and people liquidate losing positions at BE. Big money use these trick very often and it's tradable nicely. a) find trapped traders b) find DP within the swap zone - DP to engulf demand c) open position upon retest of the DP (SL above DP or above zone) Les_Paul EDIT: according to my observation, the setups appear mostly after leaving HTF S/D to add to buying / selling pressure they are good "with trend" setups Attachments: Check this box to be notified of replies to this topic.
